A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it.     "- Explore: Read-only search agent for broad fan-out searches — when answering means sweeping many files, directories, or naming conventions and you only need the conclusion, not the file dumps. Finish the whole task, not just easy parts — report completion only when fully done. Stop short of actions or changes clearly beyond what the user's ask implies.\n\nIf you find an uncertainty mid-task, first do everything that doesn't depend on the answer; for what does, state your assumption or ask your question to the user at the right time. Start a new sentence instead of joining clauses with a semicolon.\n- No em-dashes, no parentheticals, no arrows.\n- State facts and conclusions. No docs skill or guide text loaded → `guide( items = [\"topic.index\"] )` alone before any docs call but a doc's birth. Make a doc here — not a local file, even when coding — only when the user asks for one, and make it FIRST: the turn's first tool call is its skeleton (title, byline, a `pending` block per section) — a reflex: send it before any search, file read, plan, `guide` or thinking it through; think once it is open — `batch( container = {\"kind\":\"project\",\"create\":{\"name\":\"<title>\",\"doc\":{\"blocks\":{\"asof\":{\"type\":\"date\",\"value\":\"<today>\"},\"me\":{\"type\":\"mention\",\"user\":\"me\"},\"s1\":{\"type\":\"pending\",\"intent\":\"Goals: the three outcomes this quarter commits to\"},\"s2\":{…}},\"markdown\":\"# <title>\\n\\n<?claude block asof?> · <?claude block me?>\\n\\n<?claude block s1?>\\n\\n<?claude block s2?>\"}}}, batch = [] )` (`<?claude block k?>` ↔ `blocks.k`); its ack links the doc → `open` it with your Artifact tool (none → start your next message with the link, once); they're likely watching it fill — keep them posted in a short line naming what you're on (outline up; now <topic>); findings go in the doc, not chat; then `guide( items = [\"topic.index\"] )`, research, and fill each section: `replace` its pending id with `## <heading>` + body; end with one line + the link, never the document. 21	  | "offline"

65	    }): Promise<{ data: unknown; lastEventId: string | null }>;
66	    open(input: {

84	  updateScopedState(

96	};
97	
98	type ScheduleHandle = unknown;
99	
100	/**
101	 * How long a dropped event stream may stay dropped before the UI calls it
102	 * degraded.
103	 *
104	 * `runtime.events_reconnecting` means the browser's own `EventSource` is
105	 * already retrying, which it does after every routine blip — a backgrounded
106	 * tab, a sleeping laptop, a proxy closing an idle connection. Painting
107	 * "Connection degraded" on the first such error made the banner near-permanent
108	 * for connections that were in fact fine, and the reconnect it scheduled tore
109	 * down streams the browser had already healed, which produced the next blip.
110	 */
111	const STREAM_DEGRADE_GRACE_MS = 4_000;
112	
113	export function calculateRunnerReconnectDelay(attempt: number, random = Math.random()) {
114	  const exponential = 1_000 * 2 ** Math.max(0, Math.floor(attempt));
115	  const jittered = exponential * (0.5 + Math.max(0, Math.min(1, random)));
116	  return Math.min(300_000, Math.max(250, Math.round(jittered)));
117	}
118	
119	function normalizeRuntimeError(error: unknown): RuntimeApiError {
120	  if (error && typeof error === "object") {
121	    const candidate = error as Partial<RuntimeApiError> & { status?: unknown };
122	    const status = typeof candidate.status === "number" ? candidate.status : null;
123	    return {
124	      code: typeof candidate.code === "string"
125	        ? candidate.code
126	        : status
127	          ? `runtime.http_${status}`
128	          : "runtime.connection_failed",
129	      message: typeof candidate.message === "string"
130	        ? candidate.message
131	        : "Server connection failed.",
132	      ...(candidate.details !== undefined ? { details: candidate.details } : {}),
133	    };
134	  }
135	  return {
136	    code: "runtime.connection_failed",
137	    message: String(error),
138	  };
139	}
140	
141	function statusForError(error: RuntimeApiError): RunnerConnectionStatus {
142	  const code = error.code.toLowerCase();
143	  if (
144	    code.includes("401")
145	    || code.includes("unauthorized")
146	    || code.includes("auth")
147	    || code.includes("login")
148	  ) {
149	    return "needs-reauth";
150	  }
151	  if (code.includes("tls") || code.includes("certificate")) {
152	    return "tls-untrusted";
153	  }
154	  if (code.includes("deferred")) {
155	    return "deferred";
156	  }
157	  return "offline";
158	}
159	
160	function isBootstrapPayload(value: unknown): value is {
161	  runner: RunnerBootstrapIdentity;
162	  initialEventState?: Record<string, unknown> | null;
163	  initialLastEventId?: string | null;
164	  initialQueries?: {
165	    session?: {
166	      enabled?: boolean;
167	      authenticated?: boolean;
168	    } | null;
169	  };
170	} {
171	  if (!value || typeof value !== "object") {
172	    return false;
173	  }
174	  const runner = (value as { runner?: unknown }).runner;
175	  return Boolean(
176	    runner
177	    && typeof runner === "object"
178	    && typeof (runner as { runnerInstanceId?: unknown }).runnerInstanceId === "string"
179	    && typeof (runner as { name?: unknown }).name === "string",
180	  );
181	}
182	
183	export class BoundedRunnerPreviewCache<T> {
184	  private readonly entries = new Map<string, T>();
185	  constructor(private readonly maximumEntries = 100) {}
186	
187	  get(key: string) {
188	    const value = this.entries.get(key);
189	    if (value === undefined) return undefined;
190	    this.entries.delete(key);
191	    this.entries.set(key, value);
192	    return value;
193	  }
194	
195	  set(key: string, value: T) {
196	    this.entries.delete(key);
197	    this.entries.set(key, value);
198	    while (this.entries.size > this.maximumEntries) {
199	      const oldest = this.entries.keys().next().value;
200	      if (oldest === undefined) break;
201	      this.entries.delete(oldest);
202	    }
203	  }
204	
205	  clear() {
206	    this.entries.clear();
207	  }
208	
209	  get size() {
210	    return this.entries.size;
211	  }
212	}

214	export class RunnerConnection extends StateManager<RunnerConnectionSnapshot> {
215	  private profile: RunnerProfile;
216	  private readonly runtimeFactory: (profile: RunnerProfile) => Promise<RunnerConnectionRuntime>;
217	  private readonly persistence: RunnerConnectionPersistence;
218	  private readonly queryClient: QueryClient;
219	  private readonly now: () => number;
220	  private readonly schedule: (callback: () => void, delay: number) => ScheduleHandle;
221	  private readonly cancelSchedule: (handle: ScheduleHandle) => void;
222	  private readonly random: () => number;
223	  private readonly onIdentity?: (connection: RunnerConnection) => void;
224	  private runtime: RunnerConnectionRuntime | null = null;
225	  private mainStream: RuntimeSubscription | null = null;
226	  private readonly terminalStreams = new Map<string, RuntimeSubscription>();
227	  private retryTimer: ScheduleHandle | null = null;
228	  private retryAttempt = 0;
229	  private degradeTimer: ScheduleHandle | null = null;
230	  /**
231	   * Set by any stream drop, cleared when the stream reopens. Tracked apart from
232	   * the visible status because the grace period deliberately keeps a brief drop
233	   * out of the UI, while requests issued during that drop still failed and
234	   * still need retrying once the stream is back.
235	   */
236	  private streamInterrupted = false;
237	  private readonly streamDegradeGraceMs: number;
238	  private generation = 0;
239	  private started = false;
240	  private connecting: Promise<void> | null = null;
241	  private resyncing = false;
242	  private cursor: string | null;
243	  private readonly resyncTimes: number[] = [];
244	  readonly previewCache = new BoundedRunnerPreviewCache<unknown>();
245	
246	  constructor(options: {
247	    profile: RunnerProfile;
248	    runtimeFactory: (profile: RunnerProfile) => Promise<RunnerConnectionRuntime>;
249	    persistence: RunnerConnectionPersistence;
250	    queryClient?: QueryClient;
251	    now?: () => number;
252	    schedule?: (callback: () => void, delay: number) => ScheduleHandle;
253	    cancelSchedule?: (handle: ScheduleHandle) => void;
254	    random?: () => number;
255	    streamDegradeGraceMs?: number;
256	    onIdentity?: (connection: RunnerConnection) => void;
257	  }) {
258	    super({
259	      profileId: options.profile.id,
260	      status: "offline",
261	      active: false,
262	      runnerInstanceId: options.profile.runnerInstanceId,
263	      runnerName: options.profile.label,
264	      observedRunnerInstanceId: null,
265	      lastError: null,
266	      retryAt: null,
267	      resyncCount: 0,
268	      snapshot: null,
269	      bootstrap: null,
270	    });
271	    this.profile = options.profile;
272	    this.runtimeFactory = options.runtimeFactory;
273	    this.persistence = options.persistence;
274	    this.queryClient = options.queryClient ?? new QueryClient();
275	    this.now = options.now ?? Date.now;
276	    this.schedule = options.schedule ?? ((callback, delay) => setTimeout(callback, delay));
277	    this.cancelSchedule = options.cancelSchedule ?? ((handle) => {
278	      clearTimeout(handle as ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>);
279	    });
280	    this.random = options.random ?? Math.random;
281	    this.streamDegradeGraceMs = options.streamDegradeGraceMs ?? STREAM_DEGRADE_GRACE_MS;
282	    this.onIdentity = options.onIdentity;
283	    this.cursor = options.persistence.getScopedState(options.profile.id).cursors.events || null;
284	  }
285	
286	  getQueryClient() {
287	    return this.queryClient;
288	  }
289	
290	  getRuntimeAPIs() {
291	    return this.runtime?.apis ?? null;
292	  }
293	
294	  async authenticateWithPassword(input: { password: string; label: string }) {
295	    this.profile = this.persistence.getProfile(this.profile.id) ?? this.profile;
296	    const profile = this.profile;
297	    const generation = ++this.generation;
298	    this.clearRetry();
299	    const runtime = this.runtime ?? await this.runtimeFactory(profile);
300	    if (
301	      generation !== this.generation
302	      || (this.persistence.getProfile(profile.id) ?? profile).baseUrl !== profile.baseUrl
303	      || !runtime.apis
304	    ) {
305	      throw { code: "runner.error.generic" };
306	    }
307	    await runtime.apis.auth.login(input);
308	    if (
309	      generation !== this.generation
310	      || (this.persistence.getProfile(profile.id) ?? profile).baseUrl !== profile.baseUrl
311	    ) {
312	      throw { code: "runner.error.generic" };
313	    }
314	    await this.retry();
315	  }
316	
317	  updateProfile(nextProfile: RunnerProfile) {
318	    const authenticationChanged = (
319	      nextProfile.baseUrl !== this.profile.baseUrl
320	      || nextProfile.credentialRef !== this.profile.credentialRef
321	      || nextProfile.authTransport !== this.profile.authTransport
322	    );
323	    this.profile = nextProfile;
324	    if (!authenticationChanged || !this.started) {
325	      return;
326	    }
327	    this.generation += 1;
328	    this.mainStream?.close();
329	    this.mainStream = null;
330	    this.closeTerminalStreams();
331	    this.runtime = null;
332	    void this.connect();
333	  }
334	
335	  queryKey(...parts: readonly unknown[]) {
336	    return [this.getSnapshot().runnerInstanceId ?? this.profile.id, ...parts] as const;
337	  }
338	
339	  async start() {
340	    if (this.started) {
341	      return this.connecting ?? Promise.resolve();
342	    }
343	    this.started = true;
344	    return this.connect();
345	  }
346	
347	  retry() {
348	    if (!this.started) {
349	      return this.start();
350	    }
351	    return this.connect();
352	  }
353	
354	  private async connect() {
355	    const generation = ++this.generation;
356	    this.clearRetry();
357	    this.patch({
358	      status: "connecting",
359	      retryAt: null,
360	      lastError: null,
361	    });
362	    const operation = this.connectGeneration(generation);
363	    this.connecting = operation;
364	    try {
365	      await operation;
366	    } finally {
367	      if (this.connecting === operation) {
368	        this.connecting = null;
369	      }
370	    }
371	  }
372	
373	  private async connectGeneration(generation: number) {
374	    try {
375	      this.profile = this.persistence.getProfile(this.profile.id) ?? this.profile;
376	      const runtime = await this.runtimeFactory(this.profile);
377	      const loadCompatibleBootstrap = async () => {
378	        const candidate = await runtime.bootstrap.load({});
379	        if (!this.isCurrent(generation)) return null;
380	        if (!isBootstrapPayload(candidate)) {
381	          throw {
382	            code: "runtime.bootstrap_invalid",
383	            message: "Server bootstrap response is invalid.",
384	          };
385	        }
386	        const compatibility = assessApiCompatibility(candidate.runner);
387	        if (!compatibility.compatible) {
388	          this.patch({
389	            status: "incompatible",
390	            observedRunnerInstanceId: candidate.runner.runnerInstanceId,
391	            lastError: {
392	              code: "runtime.incompatible",
393	              message: "Server API revisions are incompatible.",
394	            },
395	          });
396	          return null;
397	        }
398	        return candidate;
399	      };
400	
401	      let payload = await loadCompatibleBootstrap();
402	      if (!payload) return;
403	      const session = payload.initialQueries?.session;
404	      if (
405	        this.profile.isSameOrigin
406	        && this.profile.savedPassword
407	        && session?.enabled
408	        && !session.authenticated
409	        && runtime.apis
410	      ) {
411	        await runtime.apis.auth.login({
412	          password: [REDACTED]
413	          label: "Browser session",
414	        });
415	        if (!this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
416	        payload = await loadCompatibleBootstrap();
417	        if (!payload) return;
418	      }
419	      const identity = await this.persistence.learnIdentity(
420	        this.profile.id,
421	        payload.runner.runnerInstanceId,
422	        {
423	          validSameOriginSession: this.profile.isSameOrigin,
424	        },
425	      );
426	      if (!this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
427	      if (identity.status === "identity_mismatch") {
428	        this.patch({
429	          status: "identity-mismatch",
430	          observedRunnerInstanceId: identity.observedRunnerInstanceId,
431	          lastError: {
432	            code: "runner.identity_mismatch",
433	            message: "The endpoint reports a different server identity.",
434	          },
435	        });
436	        return;
437	      }
438	      this.profile = this.persistence.getProfile(identity.profileId) ?? {
439	        ...this.profile,
440	        runnerInstanceId: payload.runner.runnerInstanceId,
441	      };
442	      this.runtime = runtime;
443	      this.retryAttempt = 0;
444	      if (payload.initialLastEventId) {
445	        await this.persistCursor(payload.initialLastEventId);
446	      }
447	      this.patch({
448	        status: payload.runner.readinessState === "stopping"
449	          ? "runner-stopping"
450	          : "online",
451	        runnerInstanceId: payload.runner.runnerInstanceId,
452	        runnerName: payload.runner.name,
453	        observedRunnerInstanceId: null,
454	        snapshot: payload.initialEventState ?? null,
455	        bootstrap: payload as HomeBootstrapPayload,
456	        lastError: null,
457	        retryAt: null,
458	      });
459	      this.onIdentity?.(this);
460	      const authenticatedSession = payload.initialQueries?.session;
461	      if (authenticatedSession?.enabled && !authenticatedSession.authenticated) {
462	        this.patch({
463	          status: "needs-reauth",
464	          lastError: {
465	            code: "runtime.http_401",
466	            message: "Server authorization is required.",
467	          },
468	        });
469	      } else if (payload.runner.readinessState !== "stopping") {
470	        this.openMainStream(generation);
471	      }
472	    } catch (error) {
473	      if (!this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
474	      this.handleConnectionError(normalizeRuntimeError(error));
475	    }
476	  }
477	
478	  private isCurrent(generation: number) {
479	    return this.started && generation === this.generation;
480	  }
481	
482	  private openMainStream(generation: number) {
483	    if (!this.runtime || !this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
484	    this.mainStream?.close();
485	    this.mainStream = this.runtime.events.open({
486	      snapshot: false,
487	      runId: null,
488	      lastEventId: this.cursor,
489	    }, {
490	      onOpen: () => {
491	        if (!this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
492	        this.retryAttempt = 0;
493	        this.clearRetry();
494	        this.clearDegradeTimer();
495	        const wasInterrupted = this.streamInterrupted
496	          || this.getSnapshot().status !== "online";
497	        this.streamInterrupted = false;
498	        if (this.getSnapshot().status !== "runner-stopping") {
499	          this.patch({ status: "online", retryAt: null, lastError: null });
500	        }
501	        // Requests that failed while this runner was unreachable keep their
502	        // error until something fetches them again, and nothing else does.
503	        // Keyed on the interruption rather than the visible status, so a drop
504	        // short enough to stay inside the grace period still clears them.
505	        if (wasInterrupted) {
506	          void refetchFailedQueries(this.queryClient);
507	        }
508	      },
509	      onEvent: (event) => {
510	        if (this.isCurrent(generation)) {
511	          void this.handleStreamEvent(event, generation);
512	        }
513	      },
514	      onError: (error) => {
515	        if (this.isCurrent(generation)) {
516	          if (error.code === "runtime.events_reconnecting") {
517	            if (this.getSnapshot().status !== "runner-stopping") {
518	              this.noteStreamInterrupted(error, generation);
519	            }
520	            return;
521	          }
522	          this.handleConnectionError(error);
523	        }
524	      },
525	    });
526	  }
527	
528	  private async handleStreamEvent(event: unknown, generation: number) {
529	    if (!event || typeof event !== "object") return;
530	    const frame = event as {
531	      kind?: string;
532	      payload?: unknown;
533	      lastEventId?: string | null;
534	    };
535	    if (frame.kind === "stream.resync_required") {
536	      await this.handleResync(generation);
537	      return;
538	    }
539	    if (frame.kind === "runner.stopping") {
540	      this.patch({ status: "runner-stopping" });
541	      return;
542	    }
543	    if (frame.kind === "auth.session_revoked") {
544	      this.patch({
545	        status: "needs-reauth",
546	        lastError: {
547	          code: "runtime.http_401",
548	          message: "Server authorization is required.",
549	        },
550	        retryAt: null,
551	      });
552	      this.mainStream?.close();
553	      this.mainStream = null;
554	      this.closeTerminalStreams();
555	      return;
556	    }
557	    if (frame.kind === "runner.renamed") {
558	      const name = (frame as { name?: unknown }).name;
559	      if (typeof name === "string" && name.trim()) {
560	        this.patch({ runnerName: name.trim() });
561	      }
562	      return;
563	    }
564	    if (frame.kind === "runner.rekeyed") {
565	      const observed = (frame as { runnerInstanceId?: unknown }).runnerInstanceId;
566	      if (
567	        typeof observed === "string"
568	        && observed
569	        && observed !== this.getSnapshot().runnerInstanceId
570	      ) {
571	        this.patch({
572	          status: "identity-mismatch",
573	          observedRunnerInstanceId: observed,
574	          lastError: {
575	            code: "runner.identity_mismatch",
576	            message: "The server was rekeyed and needs identity confirmation.",
577	          },
578	        });
579	      }
580	      return;
581	    }
582	    if (frame.lastEventId) {
583	      await this.persistCursor(frame.lastEventId);
584	    }
585	    if (frame.kind === "update" && frame.payload && typeof frame.payload === "object") {
586	      this.patch({ snapshot: frame.payload as Record<string, unknown> });
587	    }
588	  }
589	
590	  private async persistCursor(cursor: string) {
591	    this.cursor = cursor || null;
592	    await this.persistence.updateScopedState(this.profile.id, {
593	      cursors: { events: cursor },
594	    });
595	  }
596	
597	  private async handleResync(generation: number) {
598	    if (!this.runtime || !this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
599	    const now = this.now();
600	    this.resyncTimes.push(now);
601	    while (this.resyncTimes[0] !== undefined && now - this.resyncTimes[0] > 5 * 60_000) {
602	      this.resyncTimes.shift();
603	    }
604	    this.patch({ resyncCount: this.resyncTimes.length });
605	    if (this.resyncTimes.length > 3) {
606	      this.mainStream?.close();
607	      this.mainStream = null;
608	      await this.persistCursor("");
609	      this.patch({
610	        status: "degraded",
611	        lastError: {
612	          code: "runtime.resync_storm",
613	          message: "Repeated stream resynchronization entered backoff.",
614	        },
615	      });
616	      this.scheduleReconnect();
617	      return;
618	    }
619	    if (this.resyncing) return;
620	    this.resyncing = true;
621	    await this.persistCursor("");
622	    this.mainStream?.close();
623	    this.mainStream = null;
624	    this.patch({ status: "resync" });
625	    try {
626	      const result = await this.runtime.events.snapshot({ persisted: true });
627	      if (!this.isCurrent(generation)) return;
628	      if (this.getSnapshot().status === "degraded") return;
629	      if (result.lastEventId) {
630	        await this.persistCursor(result.lastEventId);
631	      }
632	      if (result.data && typeof result.data === "object") {
633	        this.patch({ snapshot: result.data as Record<string, unknown> });
634	      }
635	      this.openMainStream(generation);
636	      this.patch({ status: "online" });
637	    } catch (error) {
638	      if (this.isCurrent(generation)) {
639	        this.handleConnectionError(normalizeRuntimeError(error));
640	      }
641	    } finally {
642	      this.resyncing = false;
643	    }
644	  }
645	
646	  /**
647	   * Record a stream drop the browser is already retrying, and only escalate to
648	   * a visible "degraded" state — and our own reconnect — if it has not come
649	   * back by the time the grace period expires. Escalating immediately meant
650	   * competing with the browser's retry: our reconnect closed the freshly healed
651	   * stream, that close surfaced as another transient error, and the two kept
652	   * each other going.
653	   */
654	  private noteStreamInterrupted(error: RuntimeApiError, generation: number) {
655	    this.streamInterrupted = true;
656	    if (this.degradeTimer) return;
657	    this.degradeTimer = this.schedule(() => {
658	      this.degradeTimer = null;
659	      if (!this.isCurrent(generation) || !this.streamInterrupted) return;
660	      if (this.getSnapshot().status === "runner-stopping") return;
661	      this.patch({ status: "degraded", lastError: error, retryAt: null });
662	      this.scheduleReconnect();
663	    }, this.streamDegradeGraceMs);
664	  }
665	
666	  private clearDegradeTimer() {
667	    if (!this.degradeTimer) return;
668	    this.cancelSchedule(this.degradeTimer);
669	    this.degradeTimer = null;
670	  }
671	
672	  private handleConnectionError(error: RuntimeApiError) {
673	    const status = statusForError(error);
674	    this.clearDegradeTimer();
675	    this.streamInterrupted = true;
676	    this.patch({ status, lastError: error });
677	    this.mainStream?.close();
678	    this.mainStream = null;
679	    if (status === "offline") {
680	      this.scheduleReconnect();
681	    }
682	  }
683	
684	  private scheduleReconnect() {
685	    if (!this.started || this.retryTimer) return;
686	    const delay = calculateRunnerReconnectDelay(this.retryAttempt, this.random());
687	    this.retryAttempt += 1;
688	    this.patch({ retryAt: this.now() + delay });
689	    this.retryTimer = this.schedule(() => {
690	      this.retryTimer = null;
691	      if (this.started) {
692	        void this.connect();
693	      }
694	    }, delay);
695	  }
696	
697	  private clearRetry() {
698	    if (!this.retryTimer) return;
699	    this.cancelSchedule(this.retryTimer);
700	    this.retryTimer = null;
701	  }
702	
703	  stop() {
704	    this.started = false;
705	    this.generation += 1;
706	    this.clearRetry();
707	    this.clearDegradeTimer();
708	    this.streamInterrupted = false;
709	    this.mainStream?.close();
710	    this.mainStream = null;
711	    this.closeTerminalStreams();
712	    this.runtime = null;
713	    this.patch({
714	      status: "offline",
715	      retryAt: null,
716	    });
717	  }
718	
719	  setActive(active: boolean) {
720	    this.patch({ active });
721	    if (!active) {
722	      this.closeTerminalStreams();
723	    }
724	  }
725	
726	  openTerminalStream(
727	    terminalId: string,
728	    handlers: RunnerStreamHandlers,
729	    lastEventId?: string | null,
730	  ) {
731	    if (!this.getSnapshot().active || !this.runtime) {
732	      throw new Error("Terminal streams are available only on the active server.");
733	    }
734	    this.terminalStreams.get(terminalId)?.close();
735	    const subscription = this.runtime.terminals.openStream(
736	      { terminalId, lastEventId },
737	      handlers,
738	    );
739	    this.terminalStreams.set(terminalId, subscription);
740	    return {
741	      close: () => {
742	        if (this.terminalStreams.get(terminalId) === subscription) {
743	          this.terminalStreams.delete(terminalId);
744	        }
745	        subscription.close();
746	      },
747	    };
748	  }
749	
750	  private closeTerminalStreams() {
751	    for (const subscription of this.terminalStreams.values()) {
752	      subscription.close();
753	    }
754	    this.terminalStreams.clear();
755	  }
756	}

183	export class LiveEventConnectionManager {
184	  private readonly selectedRunId?: string | null;
185	  private readonly events: Pick<RuntimeAPIs["events"], "snapshot" | "open">;
186	  private readonly getSnapshotChecksum?: () => string | null | undefined;
187	  private readonly workerEntries: Pick<typeof workerEntriesManager, "onKnownSeqs" | "onStreamResync" | "onWakeUp">;
188	  private readonly planManager: Pick<AcpPlanManager, "onKnownSeqs" | "onStreamResync" | "onWakeUp">;
189	  private readonly sidebarWorkerActivity: Pick<SidebarWorkerActivityManager, "onKnownSeqs" | "onWakeUp">;
190	  private readonly applyUpdate: (state: EventStreamState) => void;
191	  private readonly applyGoalEvent?: (snapshot: GoalSnapshot, eventKey: string | null) => boolean;
192	  private readonly reportError: (error: AppErrorDescriptor) => void;
193	  private readonly onStreamResync?: () => void;
194	  private readonly onConnectionRestored?: () => void;
195	  private readonly fallbackIntervalMs: number;
196	  private readonly fallbackCooldownMs: number;
197	  private readonly snapshotValidationIntervalMs: number | null;
198	  private eventSource: RuntimeSubscription | null = null;
199	  private fallbackTimer: ReturnType<typeof setInterval> | null = null;
200	  private snapshotValidationTimer: ReturnType<typeof setInterval> | null = null;
201	  private active = false;
202	  private pollingSnapshot = false;
203	  private snapshotPollPromise: Promise<boolean> | null = null;
204	  private reconnectingAfterResync = false;
205	  private connectionDegraded = false;
206	  private lastSnapshotPollAt = 0;
207	  private readonly cursor: LiveEventCursorManager;
208	  private connectionGeneration = 0;
209	
210	  constructor(options: LiveEventConnectionManagerOptions) {
211	    this.selectedRunId = options.selectedRunId;
212	    this.cursor = options.cursor ?? new LiveEventCursorManager(options.initialLastEventId);
213	    this.cursor.advance(options.initialLastEventId);
214	    this.events = options.events ?? createLegacyLiveEventAPIs({
215	      EventSourceConstructor: options.EventSourceConstructor,
216	      requestJson: options.requestJson,
217	      requestSnapshot: options.requestSnapshot,
218	    });
219	    this.getSnapshotChecksum = options.getSnapshotChecksum;
220	    this.workerEntries = options.workerEntries ?? workerEntriesManager;
221	    this.planManager = options.planManager ?? acpPlanManager;
222	    this.sidebarWorkerActivity = options.sidebarWorkerActivity ?? sidebarWorkerActivityManager;
223	    this.applyUpdate = options.applyUpdate;
224	    this.applyGoalEvent = options.applyGoalEvent;
225	    this.reportError = options.reportError;
226	    this.onStreamResync = options.onStreamResync;
227	    this.onConnectionRestored = options.onConnectionRestored;
228	    this.fallbackIntervalMs = options.fallbackIntervalMs ?? SNAPSHOT_FALLBACK_INTERVAL_MS;
229	    this.fallbackCooldownMs = options.fallbackCooldownMs ?? SNAPSHOT_FALLBACK_COOLDOWN_MS;
230	    this.snapshotValidationIntervalMs = options.snapshotValidationIntervalMs === undefined
231	      ? SNAPSHOT_VALIDATION_INTERVAL_MS
232	      : options.snapshotValidationIntervalMs;
233	  }
234	
235	  start() {
236	    if (this.active) {
237	      return;
238	    }
239	
240	    this.active = true;
241	    this.openEventSource();
242	
243	    this.startSnapshotValidation();
244	    void this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
245	  }
246	
247	  private openEventSource() {
248	    // `EventSource` reconnects itself after a drop and fires `onopen` again on
249	    // the same object, so this distinguishes the first open from a recovery.
250	    let opened = false;
251	    this.eventSource = this.events.open({
252	      snapshot: false,
253	      runId: this.selectedRunId,
254	      lastEventId: this.cursor.getCurrent(),
255	    }, {
256	      onOpen: () => {
257	        const reopened = opened;
258	        opened = true;
259	        this.stopFallbackPolling();
260	        this.markConnectionHealthy();
261	        if (reopened) {
262	          // A resumed stream replays named events from our cursor and then
263	          // carries only the rows that change from here on: the server diffs
264	          // the catalog against what *this* connection has delivered, and a
265	          // fresh connection starts that baseline from the rows it is about to
266	          // skip. So a run that reached a terminal state while the stream was
267	          // down changed exactly once, against no baseline, and is never sent
268	          // again — the sidebar keeps the pre-drop "running" row and spins its
269	          // working indicator for the life of the page. The poll issued from
270	          // `onError` was made while the runtime was still unreachable, so it
271	          // cannot stand in for this one. Reconcile against a complete
272	          // snapshot; an unchanged catalog costs a checksum round-trip.
273	          void this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
274	        }
275	      },
276	      onEvent: (event) => {
277	        const streamEvent = event as {
278	          kind?: string;
279	          payload?: unknown;
280	          lastEventId?: string | null;
281	        };
282	        if (streamEvent.kind !== "update" && streamEvent.lastEventId && !this.cursor.advance(streamEvent.lastEventId)) {
283	          return;
284	        }
285	        if (streamEvent.kind === "update") {
286	          this.handleUpdateEvent(streamEvent);
287	        } else if (streamEvent.kind === "update_error") {
288	          this.handleUpdateErrorEvent(streamEvent.payload);
289	        } else if (streamEvent.kind === "worker.entry_appended") {
290	          this.handleWorkerEntryAppended(streamEvent.payload);
291	        } else if (streamEvent.kind === "worker.plan_updated" || streamEvent.kind === "worker.plan_boundary_started") {
292	          this.handlePlanWakeUp(streamEvent.payload);
293	        } else if (streamEvent.kind?.startsWith("goal.")) {
294	          this.handleGoalEvent(streamEvent.payload);
295	        } else if (streamEvent.kind === "stream.resync_required") {
296	          void this.handleStreamResyncRequired();
297	        }
298	      },
299	      onError: () => {
300	        this.markConnectionDegraded();
301	        this.startFallbackPolling();
302	        void this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
303	      },
304	    });
305	  }
306	
307	  stop() {
308	    this.active = false;
309	    this.connectionGeneration++;
310	    this.stopFallbackPolling();
311	    this.stopSnapshotValidation();
312	    this.eventSource?.close();
313	    this.eventSource = null;
314	  }
315	
316	  private markConnectionDegraded() {
317	    this.connectionDegraded = true;
318	  }
319	
320	  /**
321	   * Requests issued during an outage fail and stay failed: a React Query error
322	   * is only cleared by a later successful fetch, and nothing else in the app
323	   * asks for one. Announcing the recovery here is what lets those callers retry
324	   * instead of leaving a dead "Failed to fetch" on screen.
325	   */
326	  private markConnectionHealthy() {
327	    if (!this.connectionDegraded) {
328	      return;
329	    }
330	
331	    this.connectionDegraded = false;
332	    this.onConnectionRestored?.();
333	  }
334	
335	  private handleUpdateEvent(event: { payload?: unknown; lastEventId?: string | null }) {
336	    try {
337	      if (event.lastEventId && !this.cursor.advance(event.lastEventId)) {
338	        return;
339	      }
340	      const data = event.payload as EventStreamState;
341	      if (!data || typeof data !== "object") {
342	        throw new TypeError("Invalid live update.");
343	      }
344	      this.stopFallbackPolling();
345	      this.markConnectionHealthy();
346	      this.applyUpdate(data);
347	      this.workerEntries.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
348	      this.planManager.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
349	      this.sidebarWorkerActivity.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
350	    } catch {
351	      this.reportError({
352	        message: "The frontend received a malformed live update payload.",
353	        source: "Events",
354	        action: "Process live updates",
355	        suggestion: "Inspect the events route response payload and server logs, then refresh the page after fixing the malformed data.",
356	      });
357	    }
358	  }
359	
360	  private handleWorkerEntryAppended(payload: unknown) {
361	    try {
362	      const data = payload as { workerId?: unknown; seq?: unknown; runId?: unknown };
363	      const workerId = typeof data.workerId === "string" ? data.workerId : null;
364	      const seq = typeof data.seq === "number" ? data.seq : null;
365	      const runId = typeof data.runId === "string" ? data.runId : null;
366	      if (!workerId || seq == null) {
367	        return;
368	      }
369	      this.workerEntries.onWakeUp({ workerId, seq });
370	      this.planManager.onWakeUp({ workerId, seq, runId });
371	      this.sidebarWorkerActivity.onWakeUp({ workerId, seq, runId });
372	    } catch {
373	      // Malformed frames are ignored — the next valid frame (or the
374	      // periodic snapshot poll) will re-sync any missed entries.
375	    }
376	  }
377	
378	  private handlePlanWakeUp(payload: unknown) {
379	    const data = payload as { workerId?: unknown; runId?: unknown; seq?: unknown };
380	    if (typeof data.workerId !== "string" || typeof data.seq !== "number") return;
381	    this.planManager.onWakeUp({
382	      workerId: data.workerId,
383	      runId: typeof data.runId === "string" ? data.runId : null,
384	      seq: data.seq,
385	    });
386	  }
387	
388	  private handleGoalEvent(payload: unknown) {
389	    if (!this.applyGoalEvent || !payload || typeof payload !== "object") return;
390	    const event = payload as { snapshot?: unknown; eventKey?: unknown };
391	    if (!event.snapshot) return;
392	    try {
393	      this.applyGoalEvent(
394	        event.snapshot as GoalSnapshot,
395	        typeof event.eventKey === "string" ? event.eventKey : null,
396	      );
397	    } catch (error) {
398	      this.reportError(withFallbackErrorContext(error, {
399	        source: "Goals",
400	        action: "Process goal update",
401	      }));
402	      void this.handleStreamResyncRequired();
403	    }
404	  }
405	
406	  private handleUpdateErrorEvent(payload: unknown) {
407	    try {
408	      this.reportError(withFallbackErrorContext(payload, {
409	        source: "Events",
410	        action: "Stream live updates",
411	      }));
412	    } catch {
413	      this.reportError({
414	        message: "The live event stream reported a malformed error payload.",
415	        source: "Events",
416	        action: "Stream live updates",
417	      });
418	    }
419	
420	    this.startFallbackPolling();
421	    void this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
422	  }
423	
424	  private async handleStreamResyncRequired() {
425	    this.workerEntries.onStreamResync();
426	    this.planManager.onStreamResync();
427	    this.onStreamResync?.();
428	    if (!this.active || this.reconnectingAfterResync) {
429	      return;
430	    }
431	
432	    this.reconnectingAfterResync = true;
433	    this.eventSource?.close();
434	    this.eventSource = null;
435	    this.cursor.clear();
436	    try {
437	      let snapshotLoaded = await this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
438	      // A bootstrap poll may have been in flight when the resync cleared its
439	      // cursor. That body correctly fails the freshness guard; immediately
440	      // issue a clean, post-clear bootstrap instead of waiting for fallback.
441	      if (this.active && !snapshotLoaded) {
442	        snapshotLoaded = await this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
443	      }
444	      if (!this.active || !snapshotLoaded) {
445	        this.startFallbackPolling();
446	        return;
447	      }
448	
449	      this.openEventSource();
450	    } finally {
451	      this.reconnectingAfterResync = false;
452	    }
453	  }
454	
455	  private startFallbackPolling() {
456	    if (this.fallbackTimer) {
457	      return;
458	    }
459	
460	    this.fallbackTimer = setInterval(() => {
461	      void this.pollSnapshot();
462	    }, this.fallbackIntervalMs);
463	  }
464	
465	  private stopFallbackPolling() {
466	    if (!this.fallbackTimer) {
467	      return;
468	    }
469	
470	    clearInterval(this.fallbackTimer);
471	    this.fallbackTimer = null;
472	  }
473	
474	  private startSnapshotValidation() {
475	    if (
476	      this.snapshotValidationTimer
477	      || this.snapshotValidationIntervalMs == null
478	      || this.snapshotValidationIntervalMs <= 0
479	    ) {
480	      return;
481	    }
482	
483	    this.snapshotValidationTimer = setInterval(() => {
484	      void this.pollSnapshot();
485	    }, this.snapshotValidationIntervalMs);
486	  }
487	
488	  private stopSnapshotValidation() {
489	    if (!this.snapshotValidationTimer) {
490	      return;
491	    }
492	
493	    clearInterval(this.snapshotValidationTimer);
494	    this.snapshotValidationTimer = null;
495	  }
496	
497	  private async pollSnapshot(options: { force?: boolean } = {}): Promise<boolean> {
498	    if (this.pollingSnapshot) {
499	      return this.snapshotPollPromise ?? Promise.resolve(false);
500	    }
501	
502	    const now = Date.now();
503	    if (
504	      !this.active
505	      || (!options.force && now - this.lastSnapshotPollAt < this.fallbackCooldownMs)
506	    ) {
507	      return false;
508	    }
509	
510	    this.lastSnapshotPollAt = now;
511	    this.pollingSnapshot = true;
512	    this.snapshotPollPromise = this.runSnapshotPoll();
513	    try {
514	      return await this.snapshotPollPromise;
515	    } finally {
516	      this.pollingSnapshot = false;
517	      this.snapshotPollPromise = null;
518	    }
519	  }
520	
521	  private async runSnapshotPoll(): Promise<boolean> {
522	    const gen = this.connectionGeneration;
523	    const cursorAtRequestStart = this.cursor.getCurrent();
524	    try {
525	      const result = await this.events.snapshot({
526	        runId: this.selectedRunId,
527	        persisted: true,
528	        checksum: this.getSnapshotChecksum?.() ?? null,
529	      }) as SnapshotPollResult;
530	      if (!this.active || gen !== this.connectionGeneration) {
531	        return false;
532	      }
533	      // The server answered, so the runtime is reachable again even if the
534	      // freshness guards below discard this particular body.
535	      this.markConnectionHealthy();
536	      // A live frame that arrived while this HTTP snapshot was being built is
537	      // newer authority for the active connection. Even an anchor allocated
538	      // after snapshot construction cannot prove the body includes that frame,
539	      // so reject the overtaken body (and its cursor hints) wholesale.
540	      if (this.cursor.getCurrent() !== cursorAtRequestStart) {
541	        return false;
542	      }
543	      const snapshotAnchor = normalizeLastEventId(result.lastEventId);
544	      const currentCursor = this.cursor.getCurrent();
545	      if (snapshotAnchor && currentCursor) {
546	        const ordering = compareEventIds(snapshotAnchor, currentCursor);
547	        if (ordering !== null && ordering < 0) return false;
548	      }
549	      if (snapshotAnchor && snapshotAnchor !== currentCursor) this.cursor.advance(snapshotAnchor);
550	      const data = result.data;
551	      if (isNotModifiedSnapshot(data)) {
552	        this.workerEntries.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
553	        this.planManager.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
554	        this.sidebarWorkerActivity.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
555	        return true;
556	      }
557	      this.applyUpdate(data);
558	      this.workerEntries.onKnownSeqs(data?.workerEntrySeqs);
559	      this.planManager.onKnownSeqs(data?.workerEntrySeqs);
560	      this.sidebarWorkerActivity.onKnownSeqs(data?.workerEntrySeqs);
561	      return true;
562	    } catch (error) {
563	      if (this.active) {
564	        this.markConnectionDegraded();
565	
566	        if (isTransientConnectivityError(error)) {
567	          return false;
568	        }
569	
570	        this.reportError(withFallbackErrorContext(error, {
571	          source: "Events",
572	          action: "Load live state snapshot",
573	        }));
574	      }
575	      return false;
576	    }
577	  }
578	
579	  private setLastEventId(lastEventId: string | number | null | undefined) {
580	    this.cursor.advance(lastEventId);
581	  }
582	}

+/**
+ * A run's activity clock only ever moves forward.
+ *
+ * "Server-authoritative" says where a payload came from, not when it was true.
+ * A snapshot body is read out of SQLite and then serialized, checksummed and
+ * sent, so a live frame built milliseconds later routinely overtakes it, and
+ * the late body rewinds every row it carries. Nothing corrects that rewind: the
+ * SSE catalog delta only re-sends rows that changed *since this connection last
+ * delivered them*, and from the server's side those rows were already handed
+ * over. So the stale value stands until some later poll happens to land — which
+ * is how a finished conversation, whose row has not changed in SQLite for an
+ * hour, walks up and down the project list while the user watches it.
+ *
+ * Pinning the high-water mark is safe because the column only advances
+ * server-side: the `runs_activity_*` triggers in `src/server/db/index.ts` each
+ * carry an explicit `last_activity_at < NEW` guard, and no client code writes
+ * the field optimistically. A genuinely newer row still wins.
+ *
+ * Deliberately narrow. `updatedAt` gets no such treatment — optimistic
+ * mutations stamp it with a local clock the server has not caught up to yet, so
+ * a server row that looks "older" there is normal and must still be applied.
+ */
+function keepLastActivityMonotonic(currentRun: RunRecord, incomingRun: RunRecord): RunRecord {
+  if (timestampMs(currentRun.lastActivityAt) <= timestampMs(incomingRun.lastActivityAt)) {
+    return incomingRun;
+  }
+
+  return { ...incomingRun, lastActivityAt: currentRun.lastActivityAt };
+}

```text
1	CRITICAL!!: 
2	- NEVER EVER EVER create a branch!!! branches are FORBIDDEN!!!
3	- NEVER EVER EVER delete the `bugdrop-screenshots` branch, locally or remotely. It is protected project history.
4	- NEVER create a worktree, UNLESS the user has specifically asked for it!
5	- NEVER delete files unless the user has specifically asked for it. If you think a file is misplaced, unrelated, or stale, that is an entirely separate request — ask first. "Looks unrelated to this task" is not authorization to delete.
6	
7	- When the user gives a UUID and asks what is going on with it, treat it as an OmniHarness conversation/session lookup: check `sqlite.db`, starting with the `runs` row for that UUID, then inspect related `workers`, `messages`, `execution_events`, queued messages, and validation/plan records as needed.
8	- To delete all conversations and associated persisted artifacts, use `scripts/delete-conversations.sh`
9	
10	Planning artifacts:
11	- When the user says “write the plan”, “write a full plan”, or “write the implementation plan”, treat that as a request to persist the complete plan to a file, not merely display it in chat.
12	- If the user gives a path, write the plan there. Otherwise use `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<kebab-case-feature-name>.md`, using the current date and a non-colliding filename.
13	- The saved file is the canonical full plan. The final response should link to it and summarize the result rather than replacing the file with a chat-only plan.
14	- This convention applies even when the current message omits the destination because “write the plan” is the project’s established shorthand for creating the plan artifact.
15	- If the same request explicitly says not to change the workspace or not to create a file, obey that more specific prohibition and ask for a destination when needed.
16	
17	Testing:
18	- After any UI/frontend change, always run `pnpm build:interface:web` before considering the work complete.
19	- When testing the app, use the already-running process if one exists instead of starting another server.
20	- The normal runner URL is `http://localhost:3050`; the Vite development interface is usually at `http://localhost:5173`.
21	- Clean up any test sessions/conversations and their associated persisted artifacts before finishing.
22	- For lifecycle / chaos-style regressions (reconnect, restart, FK-on-delete, plan-review leftover state), use `pnpm test:lifecycle`. Scenarios live under `tests/lifecycle/scenarios/` and drive the control plane via HTTP/SSE — no Chromium. To debug a specific reported bug, mirror it as a new scenario file.
23	- Read `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md` before adding new server-side state transitions. It is the spec all new code is held to.
24	- Worker conversation content (direct-control and supervisor-driven worker turns) lives in the unified worker stream — one append-only JSONL per worker at `app-data/run-data/<runId>/<workerId>.jsonl`. Persist new worker content through `appendWorkerEntry` in `src/server/workers/output-store.ts`; render it through `WorkerEntriesManager` and the `entries` prop on `Terminal`. Do NOT add a parallel persistence layer (a sibling JSONL, a new `messages.kind`, an in-memory cache that the frontend reconciles against the stream). See `docs/architecture/worker-conversation-stream.md`.
25	
26	Lifecycle observability rules (full doc: `docs/architecture/lifecycle-observability-and-testing.md`):
27	- Every server-side decision (spawn, reattach, recreate, give up, refuse, delete, fail) emits a typed named event via `emitNamedEvent` from `@/server/events/named-events`. Silent early returns and bare `catch {}` are bugs.
28	- User-relevant failures additionally emit `error.surfaced` with a stable `code` (typed union in `named-events.ts`), `surface`, and at least one relevant subject id such as `runId`/`workerId`/`conversationId`/`accountId`. Never funnel through a blanket wrapper in `api-errors.ts`.
29	- All SSE frames carry an `id:`. Clients reconnect with `Last-Event-ID`; the server replays from the ring buffer or emits `stream.resync_required`. Snapshot bootstrap is `GET /api/events?snapshot=1` (anchor id in the `x-omni-last-event-id` response header).
30	- Dev-only event log: `GET /api/events/log?since=<id>&runId=<id>` returns the ring buffer as JSON. Use this when triaging "X didn't happen" bug reports — if the event isn't there, the server didn't do the thing, and the next step is finding the silent branch.
31	- Chaos is a client-side concern. Never add fault-injection code paths to server code.
32	
33	Frontend i18n:
34	- EVERY user-facing frontend string MUST live in `shared/locales/*.json` and be rendered with the `t()` function from `@/lib/i18n`.
35	- NEVER hardcode user-facing JSX text, button labels, dialog titles, aria-labels, titles, placeholders, empty states, status labels, help text, error fallback text, or visible option labels directly in components.
36	- For every new user-facing string, add the key to `shared/locales/en.json` and every other locale file in `shared/locales/` in the same change. Use stable dotted keys like `settings.runtime.recovery` or `conversation.sidebar.search`.
37	- Components that render translated strings and need to update when the language changes MUST subscribe to the i18n manager by calling `useI18nSnapshot()` from `@/lib/i18n`; then render text with the existing `t("key", params?)` function. The hook is only for re-rendering; `t()` is the translation API.
38	- Non-React modules that produce user-visible text must still use `t("key", params?)` at the boundary where text is produced. Protocol values, storage keys, API paths, CSS classes, model ids, and database values are not translation keys unless they are displayed as copy.
39	- Do not pass translated strings through transactions or persist translated UI copy. Store stable ids/values and translate at render time.
40	
41	React Best Practices:
42	- MutableRefObject is deprecated in React; use RefObject instead.
43	- NEVER use require() to import modules. Use import instead.
44	- ALWAYS centralize all state in global single source of truth Manager classes. Components/listeners subscribe to updates from Managers and use Manager methods to update data.
45	- Always implement a custom data manager for any data structure. NEVER use separate state variables and arrays as the source of truth.
46	- NEVER send data around in transactions.
47	- Avoid using useEffect() to update state. Prefer useCallback() or Manager methods for explicit state transitions.
48	- Avoid callback hell, race conditions, and infinite loops.
49	- NEVER store string literals or UI settings for the frontend in the .env file. Use literals in files, inline arrays, or .json files instead.
50	- When debugging React: if a fix did not work, think hard about whether this is a race condition. Race conditions are common in React.
51

10	export interface WebRuntimeApiOptions {
11	  baseUrl?: string;
12	  fetchImpl?: typeof fetch;
13	  EventSourceImpl?: EventSourceConstructor;
14	  bearerToken?: string | (() => string | null);
15	}
16

24	type EventSourceConstructor = new (url: string) => RuntimeEventSource;
25	
26	function joinUrl(baseUrl: string, path: string) {
27	  if (!baseUrl) return path;
28	  return `${baseUrl.replace(/\/$/, "")}${path}`;
29	}
30	
31	export function createWebRuntimeAPIs(options: WebRuntimeApiOptions = {}): RuntimeAPIs {
32	  const fetchImpl = options.fetchImpl
33	    ?? (typeof fetch !== "undefined" ? fetch : null);
34	  if (!fetchImpl) {
35	    throw new TypeError("A fetch implementation is required.");
36	  }
37	  const baseUrl = options.baseUrl ?? "";
38	  const EventSourceCtor: EventSourceConstructor | null = options.EventSourceImpl
39	    ?? (typeof EventSource !== "undefined"
40	      ? EventSource as unknown as EventSourceConstructor
41	      : null);
42	
43	  const request = createFetchRuntimeRequest({
44	    baseUrl,
45	    fetchImpl,
46	    surface: "web",
47	    bearerToken: options.bearerToken,
48	  });
49	
50	  const domains = createRuntimeDomains({
51	    request,
52	    openEvents(path, input, handlers): RuntimeSubscription {
53	      if (!EventSourceCtor) {
54	        handlers.onError?.({
55	          code: "runtime.events_unavailable",
56	          message: "Runtime event streaming requires EventSource support.",
57	          surface: "web",
58	        });
59	        return { close() {} };
60	      }
61	
62	      let source: RuntimeEventSource | null = null;
63	      let closed = false;
64	      const emit = (event: RuntimeStreamEvent) => {
65	        handlers.onEvent(normalizeRuntimeStreamEvent(event));
66	      };
67	      const connect = async () => {
68	        let ticket: string | null = null;
69	        if (options.bearerToken) {
70	          const issued = await request("POST", "/api/auth/stream-ticket", {
71	            body: { path: new URL(path, "http://runtime.local").pathname },
72	          }) as { ticket: string };
73	          ticket = issued.ticket;
74	        }
75	        if (closed) {
76	          return;
77	        }
78	        const query = new URLSearchParams();
79	        if (ticket) {
80	          query.set("ticket", ticket);
81	        }
82	        if (input.lastEventId) {
83	          query.set("cursor", input.lastEventId);
84	        }
85	        const separator = path.includes("?") ? "&" : "?";
86	        const suffix = query.size > 0 ? `${separator}${query.toString()}` : "";
87	        const nextSource = new EventSourceCtor(
88	          joinUrl(baseUrl, `${path}${suffix}`),
89	        );
90	        source = nextSource;
91	        nextSource.onopen = () => handlers.onOpen?.();
92	        nextSource.addEventListener("message", emit);
93	        nextSource.addEventListener("update", emit);
94	        nextSource.addEventListener("data", emit);
95	        nextSource.addEventListener("exit", emit);
96	        nextSource.addEventListener("update_error", emit);
97	        nextSource.addEventListener("worker.entry_appended", emit);
98	        nextSource.addEventListener("stream.resync_required", emit);
99	        nextSource.addEventListener("auth.session_revoked", emit);
100	        nextSource.addEventListener("runner.stopping", emit);
101	        nextSource.addEventListener("runner.renamed", emit);
102	        nextSource.addEventListener("runner.rekeyed", emit);
103	        nextSource.addEventListener("worker.plan_updated", emit);
104	        nextSource.addEventListener("worker.plan_boundary_started", emit);
105	        for (const eventType of GOAL_SNAPSHOT_EVENT_TYPES) {
106	          nextSource.addEventListener(eventType, emit);
107	        }
108	        nextSource.addEventListener("error", () => {
109	          if (closed || source !== nextSource) {
110	            return;
111	          }
112	          if (nextSource.readyState !== 2) {
113	            handlers.onError?.({
114	              code: "runtime.events_reconnecting",
115	              message: "Event stream is reconnecting.",
116	              surface: "web",
117	            });
118	            return;
119	          }
120	          nextSource.close();
121	          source = null;
122	          handlers.onError?.({
123	            code: "runtime.events_failed",
124	            message: "Event stream failed.",
125	            surface: "web",
126	          });
127	        });
128	      };
129	      void connect().catch((error: unknown) => {
130	        if (closed) {
131	          return;
132	        }
133	        handlers.onError?.({
134	          code: "runtime.stream_ticket_failed",
135	          message: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
136	          surface: "web",
137	        });
138	      });
139	      return {
140	        close: () => {
141	          closed = true;
142	          source?.close();
143	        },
144	      };
145	    },
146	  });
147	  return {
148	    runtime: {
149	      surface: "web",
150	      label: "Web",
151	      supportsNativeNotifications: false,
152	      supportsEditorActions: false,
153	    },
154	    ...domains,
155	  };
156	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T11:32:32.879Z

Found it — the update snapshot frame is 1,089,414 bytes, just over the 1 MiB max queued bytes limit, so the server rejects it and sends stream.resync_required with reason frame_too_large every time, happening 40 times in the buffer window. On the client side, RunnerConnection.handleResync tracks these and flags the connection as "degraded" once resyncs exceed 3 within 5 minutes.

This explains the permanent "Connection degraded" state, since the 4-second grace period on events_reconnecting never even addresses this scenario. Worse, LiveEventConnectionManager reacts to a resync by closing the stream, polling a fresh snapshot, and reopening — but the server tries sending the same oversized update frame again, triggering another resync, creating an infinite loop that also wastes bandwidth on repeated 1MB+ snapshot polls.

I'm now considering fixes: I could try raising the maxQueuedBytes ceiling, but that's just a band-aid since the real question is why the payload ballooned to over 1MB in the first place — likely too many runs or execution events bundled in one frame. The real fix needs to prevent the stream from ever falling into this resync loop when a frame is oversized, rather than just tolerating bigger frames.

I want to check whether the opening frame on a fresh connection is supposed to omit already-known data (since the client bootstraps from a full snapshot) — the comment suggests it should act as a diff baseline rather than redeliver everything, but the frame is still hitting 1.09MB, so the bulk must come from something other than runs, maybe execution events or worker entries. I also need to track down where maxQueuedBytes is actually configured to understand whether it's a global or per-frame limit.

True correctness matters more than performance here, though I should also check why the payload is so large by fetching the snapshot endpoint and seeing which keys dominate the size, to figure out if trimming (like capping execution events) is warranted. I also need to trace the client-side resync loop to confirm the polling would keep hitting oversized snapshots, then grep for maxQueuedBytes and the oversized-frame handling to locate the actual queue implementation.

620	/**
621	 * Live SSE frames only need the selected conversation's changing catalog
622	 * records, plus any *other* run row that changed since the last frame on this
623	 * connection. The client already received the complete catalog during snapshot
624	 * bootstrap and merges this explicitly partial slice by stable ids.
625	 *
626	 * The changed-run delta is not optional politeness: the sidebar sorts every
627	 * project by `runs.lastActivityAt`, so a frame that carries only the selected
628	 * run freezes every other row's sort key until the next complete validation
629	 * poll a minute later. The list then reshuffles in bursts, and whichever
630	 * conversation the user opens is the only one with a current key — so it always
631	 * appears newest. Sending the rows that actually changed keeps ordering live
632	 * without putting the whole catalog on every frame.
633	 *
634	 * Partial frames deliberately omit the complete-catalog checksum. Otherwise
635	 * a change to an unselected run could advance the client's checksum without
636	 * delivering that run and make the next complete validation return a false
637	 * `notModified` result.

639	function buildEventStreamPayload(
640	  payload: EventPayload,
641	  options: EventPayloadOptions,

654	  // useful to the sidebar alongside the plan it points at.
655	  const streamPlanIds = new Set(runs.map((run) => run.planId).filter(Boolean));

663	    sessions: payload.sessions?.filter((session) => session.runId === selectedRunId),

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T11:33:06.676Z

The snapshot endpoint requires auth, so I need to figure out how to get a valid password or session—maybe checking for a saved password concept, an env var, or configuration file. Alternatively, I could query the sqlite database directly to pull counts like runs and execution events instead of going through the authenticated API.

Rather than measure precisely, I'm reasoning through it: RunnerConnection opens its stream with runId set to null, meaning it receives the full unfiltered catalog payload every frame—all runs, plans, workers, execution events, roughly 1.09 MB—while LiveEventConnectionManager opens a separate filtered stream tied to selectedRunId. So there are two SSE streams, and the RunnerConnection one carrying the full catalog on every rebuild is the one overflowing. For a real fix, I'm considering the bounded-byte-stream logic: a single frame larger than maxQueuedBytes can never be sent, but that cap exists to detect slow consumers, not to block legitimately large first frames. I should read through bounded-byte-stream.ts to decide whether to allow oversized frames when the queue is empty, or simply raise the byte limits.

Raising limits only masks the real issue though—the RunnerConnection stream shouldn't be resending the entire catalog every rebuild in the first place, since with an active worker that could mean 500 KB/s of redundant traffic. The lasting fix is likely extending the delta mechanism used for selected-run streams to also apply when no run is selected, rather than always returning the whole payload in buildEventStreamPayload.

But RunnerConnection just replaces its snapshot wholesale (`this.patch({ snapshot: frame.payload })`) and relies on it for run status badges and resolving active runs, so switching to a partial/delta payload would break that logic unless I add proper merge semantics there too.

I should check how auth works — probably a 401 without credentials — and instead look directly at the sqlite database to count rows across runs, execution_events, workers, etc. I also want to check buildEventPayload to see which record collections are included and whether any caps are applied.

On the client side, a frame_too_large resync shouldn't itself count as storm activity — the storm detector correctly flags a loop, but the real fix is stopping the loop server-side after frame_too_large by properly advancing lastDeliveredId and lastUpdatePayload.

Now I've confirmed that unchanged payloads won't retry, but a live worker changes its payload every few seconds, causing repeated resyncs matching the observed log pattern. Next I want to pull together the full bounded-byte-stream.ts implementation, inspect events.ts for how event records are read and capped, and check the sqlite database's table structure to understand row counts.

1	export const DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_FRAMES = 256;
2	export const DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES = 1024 * 1024;
3	
4	export interface BoundedByteStreamWriter {
5	  enqueue(chunk: Uint8Array): boolean;
6	  close(): void;
7	  error(reason: unknown): void;
8	}
9	
10	export interface BoundedByteStreamOverflow {
11	  queuedFrames: number;
12	  queuedBytes: number;
13	  rejectedBytes: number;
14	}
15	
16	export interface BoundedByteStreamOversizedFrame {
17	  rejectedBytes: number;
18	  maxQueuedBytes: number;
19	}
20	
21	export interface BoundedByteStreamOptions {
22	  start(writer: BoundedByteStreamWriter): void | Promise<void>;
23	  cancel?(reason: unknown): void | Promise<void>;
24	  onOverflow?(overflow: BoundedByteStreamOverflow): void;
25	  /**
26	   * A single frame too large to ever fit the byte budget, offered to the caller
27	   * before the stream gives up on it. A subscriber that has consumed everything
28	   * is not slow, so this is not overflow: the caller drops the frame and sends
29	   * something smaller in its place, and the connection survives. Without a
30	   * handler the frame falls through to the overflow path below.
31	   */
32	  onOversizedFrame?(frame: BoundedByteStreamOversizedFrame): void;
33	  maxQueuedFrames?: number;
34	  maxQueuedBytes?: number;
35	}
36	
37	export function createBoundedByteStream(
38	  options: BoundedByteStreamOptions,
39	): ReadableStream<Uint8Array> {
40	  const maxQueuedFrames = options.maxQueuedFrames
41	    ?? DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_FRAMES;
42	  const maxQueuedBytes = options.maxQueuedBytes
43	    ?? DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES;
44	  const queued: Uint8Array[] = [];
45	  let queuedBytes = 0;
46	  let controller: ReadableStreamDefaultController<Uint8Array> | null = null;
47	  let waitingForChunk = false;
48	  let closeRequested = false;
49	  let finished = false;
50	
51	  const writer: BoundedByteStreamWriter = {
52	    enqueue(chunk) {
53	      if (finished || closeRequested) {
54	        return false;
55	      }
56	
57	      if (waitingForChunk && controller) {
58	        waitingForChunk = false;
59	        controller.enqueue(chunk);
60	        return true;
61	      }
62	
63	      // The budget bounds a *backlog*, not one frame. A lone frame waiting on a
64	      // consumer that has taken everything else is not a backlog, and the fast
65	      // path above already hands one straight through when the consumer happens
66	      // to be parked in `pull()` — so rejecting the same frame here only because
67	      // the timing differed is what made an oversized snapshot disconnect the
68	      // subscriber on a queue holding nothing.
69	      if (queued.length === 0) {
70	        queued.push(chunk);
71	        queuedBytes += chunk.byteLength;
72	        return true;
73	      }
74	
75	      if (chunk.byteLength > maxQueuedBytes && options.onOversizedFrame) {
76	        // Never fits behind anything, however fast the subscriber drains. Let
77	        // the caller substitute a smaller frame rather than killing a
78	        // connection that is keeping up.
79	        options.onOversizedFrame({
80	          rejectedBytes: chunk.byteLength,
81	          maxQueuedBytes,
82	        });
83	        return false;
84	      }
85	
86	      if (
87	        queued.length >= maxQueuedFrames
88	        || queuedBytes + chunk.byteLength > maxQueuedBytes
89	      ) {
90	        options.onOverflow?.({
91	          queuedFrames: queued.length,
92	          queuedBytes,
93	          rejectedBytes: chunk.byteLength,
94	        });
95	        queued.length = 0;
96	        queuedBytes = 0;
97	        closeRequested = true;
98	        finished = true;
99	        controller?.close();
100	        return false;
101	      }
102	
103	      queued.push(chunk);
104	      queuedBytes += chunk.byteLength;
105	      return true;
106	    },
107	    close() {
108	      if (finished || closeRequested) {
109	        return;
110	      }
111	      closeRequested = true;
112	      if (queued.length === 0 && controller) {
113	        finished = true;
114	        controller.close();
115	      }
116	    },
117	    error(reason) {
118	      if (finished) {
119	        return;
120	      }
121	      finished = true;
122	      queued.length = 0;
123	      queuedBytes = 0;
124	      controller?.error(reason);
125	    },
126	  };
127	
128	  return new ReadableStream<Uint8Array>({
129	    start(streamController) {
130	      controller = streamController;
131	      Promise.resolve(options.start(writer)).catch((error) => {
132	        writer.error(error);
133	      });
134	    },
135	    pull(streamController) {
136	      const chunk = queued.shift();
137	      if (chunk) {
138	        queuedBytes -= chunk.byteLength;
139	        streamController.enqueue(chunk);
140	        return;
141	      }
142	      if (closeRequested) {
143	        if (!finished) {
144	          finished = true;
145	          streamController.close();
146	        }
147	        return;
148	      }
149	      waitingForChunk = true;
150	    },
151	    async cancel(reason) {
152	      if (finished) {
153	        return;
154	      }
155	      finished = true;
156	      queued.length = 0;
157	      queuedBytes = 0;
158	      await options.cancel?.(reason);
159	    },
160	  }, {
161	    highWaterMark: 0,
162	  });
163	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T11:33:52.002Z

I'm noticing the oversized rejection only triggers when the queue isn't empty—meaning a prior chunk is still waiting on the consumer. With a zero highWaterMark, pull only fires on read, so in typical Bun/Node response streaming there's often one frame already queued, like a heartbeat or named event.

Tracing where the 1.09MB payload comes from: it's mostly the 827 workers each carrying up to a 1000-char initialPrompt preview, roughly 800KB total, likely used as a sidebar title fallback in the UI. I'm now thinking through a real fix: rather than blocking any oversized frame outright, the server should allow it as long as the existing backlog (queued bytes excluding the new chunk) stays under the budget.

This makes the logic more permissive — a slow consumer still trips the disconnect once its backlog exceeds budget, but a single big frame arriving into a mostly-empty queue never causes a rejection. That effectively makes the old oversized-frame handling redundant, though I'd need to check and possibly update the existing tests that specifically cover oversized-frame behavior before removing that code path.

Removing onOversizedFrame entirely would ripple into events.ts and touch the named event too, which feels like a bigger blast radius than needed. I'm leaning toward keeping the hook but narrowing when it fires — only when a chunk alone exceeds budget and there's already backlog present — though I'm still working out the exact condition.

Actually, the cleaner semantic is to check the backlog before accepting the new chunk: if the bytes already waiting are within budget, accept it, even if that pushes backlog slightly over on this one frame. Since the next enqueue during an unresponsive consumer will then overflow and trigger disconnect, that's still a valid slow-consumer detector, just triggered one frame later — meaning the oversized-frame path becomes unnecessary and I can drop it from the bounded byte stream.

But before deleting `stream.oversized_frame` entirely, I should check whether tests still reference it, so I'll grep for that first. Separately, I'm looking at payload size for the worker catalog stream — 827 workers each carrying a 1000-char initialPrompt preview adds up, so I want to check whether the frontend actually uses that field for non-selected workers, and if not, either drop it or shrink the preview to ~120 chars to reduce the per-frame payload.

I'm also noting that RunnerConnection opens a separate full stream (runId: null) alongside LiveEventConnectionManager, which is architecturally duplicated but out of scope to fix now — I confirmed the oversized frame originated from this runId-null runner registry stream. On the client side, the resync-storm-to-degraded logic seems like the right signal for genuine loops, so I'll leave that as-is.

With the frame-size fix in place, loops caused by oversized frames should disappear, so `frame_too_large` no longer needs special exclusion from the storm detection since it won't be emitted anymore. There's still a second trigger for degraded state (transient events_reconnecting lasting over 4s), but that's already covered in the existing diff.

Next I need to independently check: whether any tests reference oversized frames or `frame_too_large`/`onOversizedFrame` handling, whether the frontend uses the worker's `initialPrompt` anywhere, and I want to actually measure the payload size by logging in with the auth credentials from the .env file and fetching the snapshot directly.

I'll grab the auth password locally, log in via curl with a cookie jar, and fetch the snapshot to measure it -- checking the auth routes file for the exact login path and body shape needed. I also still need to read through the full `buildEventPayload` function to understand how the catalog is composed.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T11:34:37.618Z

I'm sizing up the payload breakdown — about 1.16MB total, with runs accounting for roughly 600KB across 524 entries (~1.15KB each), and sessions looking like a duplicated derivative of runs data at 240KB. The top fields I've measured only account for about 120KB of that 600KB in runs, so I need to dig into the full field-by-field breakdown to find where the rest is going.

My plan is forming: first, fix the server stream with a backlog-based budget so oversized frames never trigger the resync-loop cliff — that's the real "forever" fix. Second, trim obvious payload waste like the duplicated sessions.capabilities data, but keep that scope limited since it's more of a product decision than a bug fix. Third, on the client side, the RunnerConnection resync storm should stop degrading once the oversized-frame cliff is eliminated.

I'm redefining the enqueue logic for the bounded byte stream so overflow checks apply only to what's already queued, not the incoming frame itself — meaning a single large frame is always accepted even if it pushes the backlog temporarily over budget, and overflow only triggers if a slow consumer lets the backlog itself grow too large.

With highWaterMark 0, the queue only accumulates while the consumer isn't actively pulling, so the real overflow risk happens when a big frame sits waiting for the next pull while more frames arrive in the meantime—and on a slow link, that wait extends until the previous large chunk finishes draining over the socket.

This creates a repeating overflow-disconnect-reconnect loop for slow clients, which is really just the existing "slow subscriber" behavior rather than a new bug. To make things more forgiving, I'm thinking of raising the default max queued bytes to something like 8 MiB so a single large frame doesn't immediately trigger overflow, paired with trimming the payload size itself.

Actually, with a backlog-based check, the "frame too large to ever fit" scenario no longer really applies, so I'm reconsidering whether to keep the oversized-frame handling at all — possibly removing the hook, the related event type, and its tests entirely if nothing still needs it.

I need to also check the field breakdown for runs, sessions, and plans to figure out what payload trimming is needed, and see where sessions are used on the client side, plus consider whether the RunnerConnection-level catalog stream should behave differently too.

I should check the bounded-byte-stream test file for oversized frame handling, and look at where `takeOversizedFrame` is declared alongside the related code in named-events.ts. I also want to trace `stripUnusedRunSnapshotFields` and how the frontend consumes sessions and capabilities from the event stream state.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T11:35:00.409Z

Right, the size isn't from one bloated field but from breadth — 524 runs times roughly 40 fields each, with key names alone contributing a big chunk. Trimming fields would be a larger effort, so the real fix has to happen at the stream level rather than the catalog payload itself.

My plan: implement a backlog-based budget in bounded-byte-stream, bump the default cap to 8 MiB, strip the oversized-path handling in events.ts along with its type in named-events, and update the tests accordingly. On the client side, the 4-second grace period is already handled, and I'm satisfied that resync storms and cursor eviction from ring buffer overflow will self-heal once the server stops emitting resyncs, so I won't dig further into ring buffer sizing right now. Next step is editing bounded-byte-stream.ts.

Then I need to remove the oversized-frame handling and its imports from events.ts, strip the corresponding union member and reason from named-events.ts (checking first whether "frame_too_large" is used elsewhere in a resync reason switch), update bounded-byte-stream.test.ts and confirm no other tests or docs still reference the old oversized-frame behavior, and verify the terminals route and attachments.ts aren't also touching this code path. I'll grep across src, docs, and tests for any remaining references before finalizing.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-22T11:35:00.410Z

I've confirmed the root cause and am fixing it: the stream's byte budget will now only bound the backlog rather than individual frames, so oversized catalog frames can no longer trigger the resync loop that degrades the UI.

  "new_string": "export const DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_FRAMES = 256;\n/**\n * How much may be waiting on a subscriber before it counts as slow. The budget\n * bounds the *backlog*: a frame is only refused when the bytes already waiting\n * exceed it, never because of its own size. A catalog snapshot for a few\n * hundred conversations is over 1 MiB, and refusing frames by their own size\n * meant every such snapshot was replaced with a resync instruction, the client\n * re-bootstrapped, the next snapshot was refused again, and the UI reported the\n * connection as degraded for as long as the catalog stayed large.\n */\nexport const DEFAULT_MAX_QUEUED_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024;"
}

Send the client to it rather than dropping the\n        // connection: the previous behaviour disconnected on the opening frame,\n        // the client reconnected, and the same oversized snapshot disconnected\n        // it again roughly every 1.5s for as long as the payload stayed large.\n        emitNamedEvent({\n          kind: \"stream.oversized_frame\",\n          stream: \"events\",\n          surface: context.surface,\n          rejectedBytes: dropped.rejectedBytes,\n          maxQueuedBytes: dropped.maxQueuedBytes,\n          ...(runIdScope ? { runId: runIdScope } : {}),\n        });\n        const marker = recordSnapshotMarker(\n          getEventStreamNotificationVersion(),\n          runIdScope,\n        );\n        if (!controller.enqueue(encoder.encode(\n          `id: ${marker.streamId}\\nevent: stream.resync_required\\ndata: ${\n            JSON.stringify({ reason: \"frame_too_large\" })\n          }\\n\\n`,\n        ))) {\n          streamClosed = true;\n          return false;\n        }\n        lastDeliveredId = marker.id;\n        return false;\n      };",
  "new_string": "      // A frame is refused only when this subscriber has fallen behind, and the\n      // stream is closed by then. The frame's own size is never a reason: the\n      // stream's byte budget bounds the backlog, so a catalog snapshot larger\n      // than the budget still goes out. Replacing such a snapshot with a resync\n      // instruction put the client in a loop — re-bootstrap, reconnect, receive\n      // the same oversized snapshot, resync again — that the UI reported as a\n      // degraded connection for as long as the catalog stayed large.\n      const writeFrame = (id: string, event: string, serializedData: string) => {\n        if (controller.enqueue(encoder.encode(\n          `id: ${id}\\nevent: ${event}\\ndata: ${serializedData}\\n\\n`,\n        ))) {\n          return true;\n        }\n        streamClosed = true;\n        return false;\n      };"

function ensureToken() {

}

function escapeHtml(value: string) {

    .replaceAll("<", "&lt;")
    .replaceAll(">", "&gt;")
    .replaceAll("\"", "&quot;");
}

}
